Course structure AC dated 13.06.2025 pg 207

# Syllabus common for B Tech/ MechaniMBA Tech (all programs), can use common question paper.

Essential Electronics Practices - This course empowers students to gather practical knowledge and skills using Internal Continues Assessment (ICA) throughout the semester.

Engineering Ethics - This course focuses on instilling Engineering ethics, which are taught and assessed throughout the semester.

Indian Knowledge System - This course enables students to integrate traditional indian wisdom with modem concepts.

Constitution of India - This course focuses on making the students aware of the Constitution of India, which is taught and assessed throughout the semester.

* Term Work = Viva / Presentations/ assignments/ quizes/class participation/case study/mini projecl/project proposal & review Y/review 2/progress review report eta The Term Work components are not exceeded 40% of the total ICA marks.

SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Program: B Tech / MBA Tech (All Programs)   Semester: I /II
Course: Calculus   Module Code: 702BS0C001
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal   Term End
(Hours   (Hours   (Hours   Credit   Continuous   Examinations (TEE)
per   per   per   Assessment   (Marks- 100)
week)   week)   week)   (ICA)
(Marks - 50)
3   0   1   4   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Knowledge of vector algebra, functions, limits, differentiation and integration of
functions.
Course Objective:
This course aims at providing adequate exposure to the theory and applications of Calculus. This
course will help the students achieve sound understanding of the concepts of calculus, develop
problem solving skills and apply the concepts and techniques of calculus to solve problems within
Engineering domain. This course will equip the students with intermediate to advanced level
concepts and aligned tools to help them tackle advanced mathematics and related applications.
Course Outcomes:
After completion of the course, students will be able to-
1. Understand the fundamental concepts of differential calculus for functions of one variable,
including limits, derivatives, Mean Value Theorems, Taylor and Maclaurin series, and
LHospital’s rule. (K2)
2. Solve problems involving functions of several variables using partial differentiation, Taylor
series expansion, and techniques for finding maxima and minima, including Lagrange’s
method of undetermined multipliers. (K3)
3. Use Beta and Gamma functions to solve problems in single-variable calculus. (K2)
4. Compute and interpret double and triple integrals, including change of variables and
coordinate transformations, and apply them to determine areas and volumes. (K3)
5. Examine vector functions using gradient, divergence, curl, and scalar potential and apply
fundamental vector calculus theorems such as Green’s, Stokes’, and Gauss’ theorems to
evaluate integrals. (K3)
6. Integrate concepts from differential, integral, and vector calculus to formulate and solve real-
world problems in physics, engineering, and applied sciences. (K3)
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1.   Differential Calculus of functions of one variable
Rolle’s theorem, Lagrange’s Mean value theorem, Cauchy’s Mean value   3
theorem
Convergence of Sequences and series, Taylor’s and Maclaurin’s Series   7
Expansion, Indeterminate forms, LHospital's rule.
_________________   AY 2026-27
Signature
(Head of the Department)
--- page 2 ---
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
2.   Partial Differentiation
Functions of several variables: Limits and continuity, Partial
differentiation, Taylor’s theorem of function of two variables   6
Maxima, Minima, Lagrange’s Method of Undetermined Multiplier.   4
3.   Integral Calculus of functions of one variable
Improper Integrals, Special functions: Beta and Gamma functions.   5
4.   Multiple Integrals
Double Integral, Change of order of Integration, Change of variables,
Jacobian, Application of Double Integral to find area.   6
Triple Integral, Change of variable to spherical and cylindrical co-   5
ordinates, Application of Triple Integral to find volume.
5.   Vector Calculus
Gradient, Directional Derivative, Divergence, Curl, Scalar Potential, Line
Integral, Surface Integral.   5
Greens Theorem, Stokes Theorem and Gauss Divergence Theorem.   4
Total   45
Text Books
1. B.V. Ramana, Higher Engineering Mathematics,1st Edition, McGraw Hill Education, 2017.
2. B.S. Grewal, Higher Engineering Mathematics, 44th Edition, Khanna Publishers, 2017.
Reference Books
1. G. B. Thomas, Calculus, 14th Edition, Pearson, 2018.
2. J. E. Marsden, A. Tromba, Vector Calculus, 6th Edition, W. H. Freeman & Company, New
York, 2013.
3. Erwin Kreyszig, Advanced Engineering Mathematics, 10th Edition, Wiley India, 2017.
4. T. M. Apostol, Calculus- Volume – I, 2nd Edition, Wiley Eastern, 2007.
Tutorial Work
10 to 12 Tutorial exercises based on the syllabus.
_________________   AY 2026-27

Pre-requisite: Knowledge of 12th grade level Physics
Course Objectives:
The knowledge of Physics relevant to engineering is critical for converting ideas into technology.
An understanding of Physics also helps engineers understand the working and the limitations of
existing devices and techniques, eventually leading to innovations and improvements. This
course aims to help students understand the basic concepts of physics thoroughly and lay the
foundations for the various engineering courses.
Course Outcomes:
After completion of the course, students would be able to:
1. Describe the fundamental properties of semiconductors and elucidate the operational
principles of semiconductor devices. (K2)
2. Apply interference and diffraction principles to analyze optical phenomena and diffraction
grating applications. (K3)
3. Explain the working principles of LASER technology and fiber optics, and apply them to
real-world communication and engineering problems. (K3)
4. Interpret the concepts of gradient, divergence, and curl in scalar and vector fields, and
recognize their applications in basic engineering problems related to electromagnetism. (K2)
5. Explore the properties and applications of modern engineering materials like nanomaterials,
superconductors, and dielectrics. (K3)
6. Gauge theoretical principles through laboratory experimentation, enhancing problem-
solving abilities and technical proficiency in modern physics applications. (K3)
Detailed Syllabus:
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Semiconductors Physics   6
Formation of energy bands and classification of solids into conductors,
semiconductors and insulators, direct and indirect band gap semiconductors,
fermi levels in semiconductor, energy gap and its temperature dependence.   3
Physics of semiconductor junction, hall effect and application.
2   Optics   5
Interference: Thin film interference, wedge shaped film and Newton’s rings
and their applications.
_________________   AY 2026-27
Signature
(Head of the Department)

SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Diffraction: Fraunhofer and Fresnel diffraction, Fraunhofer diffraction at   4
single slit, double slit, and multiple slits, Characteristics of diffraction grating
and its applications
3 LASER and Fiber optics   5
Introduction   to   interaction   of   radiation with   matter, Population
inversion, pumping, various modes, threshold, population inversion, Solid
state LASER, Semiconductor LASER, Gas LASER.
Introduction, optical fiber as a dielectric wave guide, total internal reflection,   4
numerical aperture and various fiber parameters, losses associated with optical
fibers, step and graded index fibers, application of optical fibers.
4   Electricity and Magnetism   5
Laws and applications of electrostatics and magnetostatics.
Maxwell’s equations and applications, introduction to waveguides.   4
5 Modern Engineering materials   4
(Introduction and basic properties of each material) Nanomaterials.
Superconductors, Dielectrics, Materials for sustainable energy applications,   5
biomaterials.
Total   45
Text Books
1. H.K Malik and A.K. Singh, Engineering Physics, 2nd Edition, Tata McGraw Hill, 2017.
Reference Books
1. Jearl Walker, David Halliday and Robert Resnick, “Fundamentals of Physics”, Wiley
India, 12th edition 2021.
2. James F. Shackelford and Madanapalli K. Muralidhara, “Introduction to Materials
Science for Engineers” Pearson Education, 8th edition, 2020.
3. A S Bandarenka, Energy Materials, A short Introduction to Functional Materials for
Energy Conversion and Storage, CRS Press, 2022.
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 practical exercises based on the syllabus.

Pre-requisite: Nil
Course Objective
Enable students to understand problem statements and solve those using basic
programming constructs. Develop skills to analyze real life problem statements and
implement using procedure-oriented paradigm.
Course Outcomes-
After completion of the course, students will be able to-
1. Comprehend problem statements, build logic and draw flowchart, (K2)
2. Develop complex logic using control structures (K3),
3. Solve problems using arrays and functions (K3),
4. Solve problems using pointers and structures (K3),
5. Solve problems related to data handling with files (K3).
Detailed Syllabus:
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction to problem solving by computational thinking:   07
Analyzing problems, problem decomposition, abstractions,
algorithms, flowcharts, basic program structure, flow of execution
of program, I/O statements, variables, data types, operators,
expressions.
2   Control structures: Conditional branching, looping, nested   08
looping, designing and applying different test cases, program
debugging.
3   Significance of data, its storage and retrieval: One-dimensional   08
and two-dimensional arrays, strings, searching, sorting (bubble
sort), basic operations on matrices.
Signature
(Head of the Department)   AY 2026-27

SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
4   Decomposing   problems   using modular programming:   08
Functions, scope of variables, parameter passing, passing arrays to
functions, inline function, macro function, recursion.
5   Programming using structures, array as instance of structures,   08
nested structures, introduction to pointers.
6   Files and Streams: handling data with the external files (text   06
and binary), read, write, search and append operations.
Total   45

1. Yashavant Kanetkar, “Let Us C: Authentic guide to C programming language”,
19th Edition, BPB publications, 2022.

Course Objective
The objective of the course is to develop students’ competency in the English language in
relation to listening, speaking and reading.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, the students will be able to –
1. Use their knowledge of vocabulary and grammar to articulate their ideas effectively
2. Demonstrate effective listening and speaking skills in oral communication situations
such as speeches, conversations, power-presentations, etc
3. Apply different reading techniques as needed to read passages effectively
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1.   Vocabulary Building through Literature
Introduction to root and affixes, Synonyms and antonyms, Idioms and   06
phrasal verbs, Commonly confused words, Words: denotation,
connotations and usage
2.   Useful Practices of Grammar
Articles and Prepositions, Subject-verb agreement, noun-pronoun
agreement, Personal Pronouns (First Person, Second Person, Third   06
Person), Modifiers – Errors in Modifiers (Misplaced, Dangling, Squinting),
Redundancies and clichés, Tenses, Parallelism, Punctuation, Sentences,
clauses and phrases, Active and passive voice,
direct and indirect speech
3.   Oral Communication   06
Listening skills, Public speaking, impromptu speaking, Situational
dialogues

Pre-requisite: NIL
Objectives:
1. To introduce to the students about the overall organization of IKS and Human Values
2. To develop an appreciation among the students the role and importance of Veda,
Vedāṅgas, Upanishad Vedas and Purāṇas
3. To show case the multi-dimensional nature of IKS and their importance in the
contemporary society
Outcomes:
After completion of the course, students would be able to:
1. Define and relate Indian Knowledge System & Human values with modern development.
(K1)
2. Demonstrate the ability to understand, ancient Indian concepts of Number Systems and
Units of Measurement, mathematical logic and mathematical proof techniques. (K2)
3. Establish understanding of the applications of ancient engineering, technology, and
architecture in the contemporary society (K2)
Detailed Syllabus:
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction:   3
Overview of IKS and Human Values; Structure; Historical Background;
Key Elements of IKS and Human Values; Importance of IKS and Human
Values
2   Indian Classic Literature:   4
Introduction and Classification of Vedas and their offerings for engineering
courses, emphasizing holistic thinking, systems design, problem-solving, and
innovations.
3   Number Systems and Units of Measurement:   4
Number systems in India - Historical evidence; Salient aspects of Indian
Mathematics; Measurements for time, distance, and weight; Indian
Mathematicians and   Scientist &   their   Contributions (Aryabhata,
Bhaskaracharya, Charaka, Brahmagupta, Varahmitra, Sushruta, Kanada,
Baudhyana, etc.)

Course: Engineering Graphics and Design   Code: 702ME0C001
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Internal   Term End

Continuous   Examinations
(Hours   (Hours per (Hours per Credit

(Marks - 50)   (Marks - 50)
(Practical
1   2   0   2   Marks Scaled to 50   Examination)
Marks Scaled to 50
Prerequisite: Nil
Course Objective
This course is aimed at providing basic understanding of the fundamentals of
Engineering Graphics; mainly visualization & conventions of drawing, the tools of
drawing and the use of drawings in engineering applications. The course has been
structured to include sufficient simulations which would aid the student in visualization
of three-dimensional objects and developing the drawing.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, the student will be able to –
1. Construct projection of lines and develop front view, top view of various types of
planes using drawing instruments
2. Apply the basic principles of solids to draw front view and top view of various types
of solids using AutoCAD software
3. Apply the basic principles of projections in converting pictorial views into
orthographic views with the help of AutoCAD software
4. Apply the basic principles of projections in converting orthographic views into
isometric drawing using AutoCAD software.
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1.   Projections of Lines   04
Introduction, lines inclined to one plane and parallel to other plane,
lines inclined to both planes. Lines limited to 1st quadrant only.
2.   Projections of Planes   03
Introduction, types of plane lamina (Triangle, rectangle, pentagon,
hexagon, circle), plane surface inclined to one reference plane
3.   Projections of Regular Solids   04
Introduction, types of solids; Projections of regular solids (prisms,
pyramids) covering those inclined to one reference plane
4.   Orthographic Projections   02
Principles of orthographic projections using 1st angle method of
projections, conventions used in quadrant formation, conversion of
isometric models to orthographic views, orthographic views of
geometrical solids and objects from industry.
AY 2026-27
--- page 12 ---
SVKM’s NMIMS Deemed-to-be University
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management and Engineering
5.   Isometric Projections   02
Principles of isometric projection - isometric views, conventions;
conversion of orthographic views to isometric models; isometrics
projections of given views.
Total   15
Text Books
1. N. D. Bhatt, V. M. Panchal and P. R. Ingle, Engineering Drawing, 54th Edition, Charotar
Publishing House, 2023.
Reference Books
1. M. B. Shah and B. C. Rana, Engineering Drawing, 2nd Edition, Pearson Education, 2014.
2. K. Venugopal and V. Prabhu Raja, Engineering Drawing + AutoCAD, 6th edition, New Age
International (P) Ltd. Publishers, 2011.
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 exercises (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus

Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme

week)   week)   week)   Credit   (Marks-50)   Examinations (TEE)

2   2   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: NIL
Course Objective
The main objective of this course is to equip the students with the ability to solve, assemble and
test simple AC and DC electrical circuits. Further, the course also enables the student to obtain
a basic understanding of the working principle and applications of electronics
devices.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students will be able to -
1. Explain the fundamental concepts of DC circuits, including Kirchhoff’s laws and network
theorems, (Understand- Level 2)
2. Interpret the principles of AC circuit analysis, including resonance and three-phase systems,
(Understand- Level 2)
3. Illustrate the working principles of transformers and different types of electrical machines,
(Understand- Level 2)
4. Explain the basic operation of analog electronic components, including rectifiers, transistors
and operational amplifiers. (Understand- Level 2)
5. Make use of logic gates and Boolean expressions for digital circuits.
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1 DC Circuits
Electrical circuit elements (R, L and C), voltage and current sources,
Kirchhoff’s current law, Kirchhoff’s voltage law, Mesh and Nodal Analysis,
6
Superposition Theorem, Thevenin’s Theorem, Norton’s Theorem.
2   AC Circuits
Generation of alternating emf, instantaneous, peak, average, RMS values
8
and other related terms, vector representation of AC quantities, Steady state
analysis of R, L, C, R-L, R-C, R-L-C series circuits, series RLC resonance. Three-
phase emf- star and delta connections, relationship between line and
phase quantities.
3   Transformers and Electrical Machines   6
Construction and working of single-phase transformer, Ideal and
Practical   transformer, Losses in transformers, Introduction to DC and AC
induction motors.
AY 2026-27
--- page 14 ---
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management and Engineering
Electronics & Telecommunication Department
4   Analog Electronics (no mathematical treatment and design)
Half and full wave rectifiers, special purpose diodes -zener diode as a voltage
regulator, BJT- working and applications, Operational amplifier- Inverting
and Non-Inverting, pin diagram, important characteristics of op-amp.   5
5   Digital Electronics
Logic gates, concept of universal logic gates; implementation of Boolean
5
expressions using logic gates, De Morgan’s theorems, simplification of
Boolean expressions using Boolean laws, application of digital circuits: half
and full adder, half and full subtractor and their K-maps.
Total   30
Text Books
1. Ravish R. Singh, Basic Electrical Engineering, 3rd edition, McGraw Hill
Education, 2018.
2. D. C. Kulshreshtha, Basic Electrical Engineering, 2nd Edition, McGraw Hill
Education, 2019.
Reference Books
1. B. L. Theraja, Fundamentals of Electrical Engineering and Electronics, 24th Edition, S.
Chand & Co, 2012.
2. Jacob Millman & Halkias, Electronic Devices & Circuits, 2nd Edition, Tata McGraw
Hill, 2013.
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 practical exercises based on the syllabus.

Course/Module: Constitution of India   Module Code: 702BS0C006
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme

1   0   0   0   Marks Scaled to 50   -
Pre-requisite:
Course Objective:
The course would enable students to get a brief introduction of the Indian Constitution and its
principles. The students would have knowledge of concept of ‘State’ and interdependencies of
its institutions vis a vis their relation with fundamental rights.
Course Outcomes:
After completion of the course, students will be able to –
1. Recall the historical evolution of India’s democratic values, emphasizing the foundational
principles of justice, equality, and liberty as enshrined in the Preamble of the Constitution.
(K1)
2. Understand the fundamental rights enshrined in the Constitution, their permissible
restrictions, and how these rights are balanced with duties, to grasp their application within
societal and professional frameworks. (K2)
3. Apply the knowledge of the structure of India's polity and the role of the Judiciary in
maintaining the basic structure of the Constitution in real-world professional contexts. (K3)
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1.   Nature, Characteristics, and Sources of the Indian Constitution
02
2.   Fundamental rights and Fundamental duties – Concept of State, Right to
Equality under Articles 14 and 15, Right to certain freedoms under Article
19, Right to Life and liberty under Article 21, Right to religion under   06
Article 25 and 26, Right to remedy under Article 32 and Fundamental
duties
3.   Indian Judiciary – Concept of Supreme Court and High Courts,
Appointment of Judges, Independence of Judiciary, Jurisdictions of   03
Supreme Court and High Courts
4.   Emergency Provisions – Concept of National Emergency under Article
352, Financial Emergency under Article 360 and President rule under
Article 356 of the Constitution
04
Total   15

Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering

2019.

Pre-requisite: None
Course Objectives:
The objective of this course is to inculcate human values in terms of ethics, to grow as a
responsible human being, and thereby be able to maintain ethical conduct in discharging one's
professional duties in the engineering career.
Course Outcomes:
After completion of the course, students would be able to:
1. Describe the Fundamental Concepts of Ethics and Philosophy of Engineering. (K1)
2. Explain the Importance of Codes of Ethics and Analyze Situations related to Ethical
Dilemmas. (K2)
3. Summarize Key Ethical Issues from Historical Case Studies and Discuss their Impact on
Engineering Practice. (K2)
4. Apply Ethical Decision-Making Models to Resolve Hypothetical Engineering Dilemmas. (K3)
Detailed Syllabus:
Unit Description   Duration
1   Introduction to the Course: Introduction to Ethics, Ethics Vs Law,   3
Introduction to Philosophy of Engineering
2   Introduction to Engineering Ethics: Codes of Ethics, Conflict of Interest,   3
Confidentiality, Whistle Blowing
3   Case Studies on Global Engineering Practices   5
4   Solving Ethical Problems: Discussion of Heroes, Journeys, and Virtue in   4
Mythology
Total   15
Text Books
1. Taebi, Behnam. Ethics and Engineering: An Introduction. Cambridge University Press, 2021.
Reference Books
1. Govindarajan, M., et al. Engineering Ethics. PHI Learning, 2013.
2. Martin, Mike W., and Schinzinge. Ethics in Engineering. Tata Mc Graw Hill Education, 2017.
